# R10 REVIEW STEPS

If applications have not been previously assigned to your GCMS homepage see Appendix A. To determine if applications have been assigned to your GCMS Homepage access your Homepage by following the steps below.

1. Log-Into GCMS→ Ensure you are on the HOME screen. If GCMS does not open directly to My Homepage, access it by clicking the Home Tab on the GCMS toolbar, and the My Homepage tab. If unable to view the Home Tab, click on the arrow at the end of the GCMS Toolbar to select Home from the dropdown.

15. Determine if the PA had a birthday between the ITA Date and Rec'd Date
### Locate the line that says Invitation to Apply in the Document field

### The Status Updated Date is the ITA date to use (NOT the Due Date)

In the above example, the Status Updated date for the ITA was March 29, 2023. The

### application Received Date was April 5, 2023. The client's birth date was August 4th.

Therefore there was no change in age, the birth date did not fall between the ITA date and

the date the file was received. If the birthdate was April 1st, there would be a change.

### If a client's DOB falls on the ITA, mark that the age hasNOT changed.

### If a client's DOB fallson the receiveddate of the application, mark the age HAS changed.

16. In GCMS, click Notes tab. Verify any notes that have been added by CSU, the call center, CPC-O, Registry, any relevant file notes regarding police certificates, or if there is an application withdrawal request. If the applicant requests a withdrawal of their EE application, the Program Assistant will promote the application and see PNP Insert for withdrawal instructions.
17. Look for potential Statutory Questions. If the client answered “Yes" to any Statutory Question, the details should be provided within the Notes. Stat Questions are identified in [Square Brackets] with additional details and explanation.

## Scroll down and verify that the client has answered all questions (Yes or No, depending

on what was provided in the notes. (match up the yes's to the notes you had reviewed, if applicable. Ex: Refused Refugee status was in notes – the yes should go with that line)

### The client is able to enter notes (in the Note tab) to respond to Statutory

Questions where they have answered “Yes” (this is not required if the client had entered Stat Questions in the Note Tab)

If the client answered yes to a question and did not provide the additional

### details (in the subtab – additional details, the Notes tab, or within incoming

correspondence), request a Schedule A form (after promotion) 0If the application only requires additional stat details; finish

#### processing the application, send request for Schedule A, and move

the file forward (you do not have to park an application for

#### additional Statutory Questions details alone)

## If the Additional Family tab in GCMS is blank (i.e. not completed by the PA,

Spouse or any eligible dependent child aged 18 or over) and file is otherwise R10 complete, request a completed and signed Additional Family Information form (IMM 5406) for the appropriate client and park the file for 7 days. Even if the information pulls in after promotion.

- When the IMM5406 is received, the program assistant will review the Additional Family Information form (IMM5406) and move the file forward if no concerns. If concerns are found (i.e. eligible family member (SP/CL/DEP child) is listed on IMM5406 form but not included on e-APR under Clients & Parties), the program assistant will park the file and send the RA an email requesting review of family members listed on IMM5406. File will remain parked until the RA provides direction on how to proceed.
- If the IMM5406 is not provided within 7 days, the program assistant will reject the file as R10 incomplete – requested documents not provided

A review of Address and Background Info is completed for all applicants on the file 18 years of age and older. Review information for the 10 years prior to the application received date OR since the applicant's 18th birthday whichever date is most recent.

Ex: Application is received on April 3, 2023. If the applicant's DOB is August 13, 1985 (age 37 at time of application), they should have provided complete address & personal histories since April 3, 2013; last 10 years.

If the applicant's DOB is August 13, 1999 (age 18 at time of application), they should have

## provided complete address & personal histories since their; since 18th birthday, August

## 13, 2017. The ADMM calculates their 18th birthdate for review automatically.

NOTE: If the correct time frames are NOT provided a Schedule A must be requested. This includes if the time at the beginning that is provided by the client is not included ex: not within the last ten years or since turning 18. Be mindful for this information, ensure the client history starts at the correct date.

REMEMBER: When assigning files to yourself, always go back to your GCMS Homepage to open the applications from the MyHomepage screen. This will avoid someone else picking the same file as you at the same time.

APPENDIX B NOC SKILL LEVEL

The NOC (National Occupation Classification) should be skill level 0, A, or B. To determine what skill level a client's NOC is, refer to the NOC Coding System below: NOC CODING SYSTEM Skill 0/Teer 0 - Any NOC that starts with a 0 Skill A/Teer 1 - When the second digit is a 0 or 1

Skill B/Teer 2 and Teer 3 - When the second digit is a 2 or 3

Skill type or level (NOC 2016) compared to TEER category (NOC 2021)

its TEER 2. If the second digit is a 3 then it's TEER 3 etc. Skill type or level (NOC 2016) compared to TEER category (NOC 2021) Skill type 0 = TEER O Skill level A = TEER 1 Skill level B = TEER 2 and TEER 3 Skill level C = TEER 4 Skill level D = TEER 5

## ***Important 2021 NOC Code changes to remember for all LOBs***

When file indicates NOC 2021:

1. New Worker (Spouse/CLP NOC to be updated) 9914 is now 09914
2.Student (Children 5 and over) 9911 is now 09911
3. Dependent (Children under 5) 9980 is now 09980
4. Specialist physicians – NOC 3111 (NOC 2016) is now Specialists in clinical and laboratory medicine – NOC 31100 (NOC 2021) (Require MCC for ECA for AlI LOB)
5. Specialist physicians – NOC 3111 (NOC 2016) is now Specialists in surgery – NOC 31101 (NOC 2021) (Require MCC for ECA for All LOB)
6. General practitioners and family physicians – NOC 3112 (NOC 2016), is now General practitioners and family physicians – NOC 31102 (NOC 2021) (Require MCC for ECA for AlI LOB)
7. Pharmacists-NOC 3131 (NOC 2016), is now Pharmacists – NOC 31120 (NOC 2021) *** If you determine your file is a rejection based on language test / benchmarks etc DO NOT REJECT – hold the file and send to the RA for review***

## Undisclosed Rep

A client may not always disclose representation. If PA lists an email address and/or most recent mailing address belonging to a Rep but there is no Rep associated under Clients & Parties, no Rep identified in Notes, and no evidence of a Rep provided under Incoming Correspondence, is a good indication the client has an undisclosed Rep. If undisclosed Rep is suspected, park the file and send an email to the RA.

Template of Undisclosed Rep email to RA: Subject of Email:Undisclosed Rep – E# Body of Email:

# Hello,

Possible undisclosed rep on application #

Most recent email:

Most recent address:

If Rep associated to the application is different than the Rep listed in the Notes or if the Rep listed in Notes Tab is different than the Rep found on the IMM5476:

Clarification is required if the client does not upload the Use of Representative form (IMM5476) appointing one Rep and cancelling another

- Park the file for 7 days and request a new IMM5476 using the following:
It appears you have two representatives associated toyour application for permanent residence. As noted onthe top of the IMM5476, you may have only one representative per application. Please provide a newly completed and signed Use of Representative (IMM5476) form appointing one representative. Also, ensure you complete Section C: Cancel the Appointment of a Representative listing the individual you do not request to represent you.

## When the new IMM5476 is received:

- We will NOT associate the new rep under the Clients & Parties tab, this will break the link with IRCC secure account for the client.
- If the PA is cancelling the Rep listed under Clients & Parties tab, we will disassociate this rep from the application If the PA is appointing the Rep listed under application notes, we will NOT associate the new rep under Clients & Parties, this will break the IRCC secure account link. For this case, add a new General note:
Rep__was cancelled and new Rep__was appointed as per IMM5476 rec'd (See edoc__)

If a Release of Information (IMM5475) is not submitted:

- Park the file for 7 days and request an IMM5475. Under outgoing correspondence send the client a request letter for the Release of Information IMM5475 It is not necessary to associate the Designated Individual to the application, review the form upon arrival to confirm it is the same Designated Individual provided in the Notes tab If after a request letter is sent and the form is not provided within the time frame provided to the client, do not reject. Proceed with the file, do not note the designated individual in the EE Eligibility note.

## APPENDIX F

## POLICE CERTIFICATES

- This review is completed for all applicants on the file 18 years of age and older
- Review the applicant's address, education/personal, and travel histories since their 18th birthday or the last 10 years (whichever comes first) to determine the Police certificates (PCs) required. PCs are required for any country where the client has resided in, or visited, for 6 months or more in a row For the applicant's current country of residence (COR), the PC must be issued no more than six months before submission of the application For countries in which the applicant no longer resides, the PC must be issued after the last time the client stayed in that country for 6 months or more in a row. NRT-No Reportable Trace, is used to refer to PCs that do not indicate a criminal record Police certificates need to be a scan of the original police certificate(s) in colour. Certified true copies and unauthorized copies are unacceptable and will result in the application being rejected as incomplete
TIP: MAKE SURE TO CHECK THE HOW-TO WEBSITE REGULARLY FOR UPDATES. YOU CAN SEE THE UPDATED DATE AT THE BOTTOM OF EACH INDIVIDUAL COUNTRY'S PAGE, IT CAN CHANGEAND BE UPDATED WITHOUT NOTIFICATION, IF YOU NOTICE A CHANGE, NOTIFY YOUR TL.

## Required PCs

PCs from some countries must be provided at the time of PR application. For specific PC information by country, refer to the How To website: [http://www.cic.gc.ca/english/information/security/police-cert/index.asp](http://www.cic.gc.ca/english/information/security/police-cert/index.asp)

## Translations of Police Certificates

If the PC is issued in a language other than English or French, the PC along with a translation completed by a certified translator is required. Unless otherwise indicated on the IRCC website. For example: the Netherlands indicates The Certificate of Good Conduct is issued in Dutch with a portion translated in English at the bottom. It is not necessary to have it translated.

Applicants are required to submit ORIGINAL PCs. Even though the client is uploading scanned copies of the documents; we cannot accept a PC stamped certified copy or copy. If a translator stamps a PC with a translation stamp (or their personal stamp etc.) the PC is still acceptable as long as the stamp does not say Copy, Certified True Copy, etc. This rule ONLY applies to PCs. (Exception-PCs issued online are acceptable even if stamped Copy, CertifiedCopy, or Certified True Copy)

mmigration, Refugees and Citizenship Canadaet Citoyenneté Canada Information disclosed under the Access to Information Act L'information divulguée en vertu de la loi sur l'accés à l’infonmation

CLIENT MUST PROVIDE:

- A scan of the original police certificate
- An English or French translation done by a certified translator
- A translators declaration/affidavit/stamp/seal
KNOWN ISSUES WITH TRANSLATIONS:

- Unable to link the client's PC with the translation-Most or all PCs have items that do not require a translations that are simply transferred to the translation from the original document (E.g. a unique number, client name and date of birth). Use these items to link the original PC to the translation of the PC in order to determine the acceptability of the document. If you are unable to link the translation with the PC, the document is not acceptable and the file is considered incomplete. If unsure, refer to the RA.
- Multi-step translations – Documents must be translated from the original language directly to English or French. If the document is translated from Language X to Language Y then to English or French, the translation is not acceptable and the file is considered incomplete, see Rejection Letter Inserts for the letter template.
- If the client does not provide a copy of the original PC, ex: PC that is not stamped copy (except electronic PCs or those from request countries), the file is incomplete.
- If the client does not provide an English or French translation of the original PC, the file is incomplete (except for those provided up front from request countries).
- If the client provides an English or French translation but no original PC, the file is incomplete (except for those provided up front from request countries).
- If the client does not provide a translator's declaration to accompany the original PC and English or French translation, the file is incomplete (sometimes the declaration can be found under other translated documents i.e. education). It could also be a stamp or seal or other indicator that the translation is official and the translator is certified.
- Translations done by an applicant's family members or the applicants themselves are not acceptable, the file is incomplete.

OFFICIAL REQUEST LETTERS – GENERAL PC LETTER VS REQUEST LETTER

- Some countries require an official request letter from IRCC in order for the client to apply for the PC. You will send request letters for these PCs if the application is otherwise R10 complete.
- In the request letter, indicate the specific country from which the PC is required and refer the client to the IRCC website for information on “How to Get a Police Certificate". Some countries only release the results directly to IRCC. In these cases, a “General Police Certificate (Template 17)” request letter, which provides ClO's mailing and courier addresses, is sent to the client In other situations where the PC is released directly to the client, a “Request for Documents (Template 2)” request letter is sent to the client. Letters for PCs are found: Start Menu>Computer>GCMS (T :)> Templates-Modèles>Office Specific Templates-Modèles spécifiques à votre bureau>Centralized Processing>CPC Sydney CIO-CTD Sydney BRCD>EE-Express Entry>EE Letters – CIO Detailed information regarding sending request letters can be found in Appendix U and the PC Request Letter Guide

s.19(1)
## RCMP CRIMINAL RECORD CHECK

RCMP screenings are submitted through GCMS if the applicants SP/CLP or DEP 18+ is/was a resident in Canada for 6 months or more in a row and is NON ACCOMPANYING.

We only require to request an RCMP criminal record check for those GCMS RCMP screening results which indicate Fingerprints Required. If results indicate Fingerprints Required; change the Status to Fingerprints Requested and send a request letter to the client for a RCMP Criminal Record Check. See Appendix U for details on generating a Request letter.

If the results indicated NRT – toggle in the ADMM RCMP Screening NRT on the Correspondence tab under the Police Pull.

## SPECIFIC PERIOD OF TIME

Some PCs only cover a specific period of time and not the full period of time that the client was in that country.

These PCs are not acceptable, the application would be considered incomplete. The program delivery instructions (PDI) states: The PCs are required upfront and mandatory for each country (except Canada) where an individual has lived for six or more months in a row. This instruction is for the purpose of the completeness check under section R10.

The PC below clearly states “during the above said period”. Therefore, if it's clear that the client resided in the country outside of the specific time period mentioned, the Program Assistant is to reject the application if the client provided time frame is not covered.

2A-2024-64537-000076

mmigration, Refugees and Citizenship Canadaet Citoyenneté Canada Information disclosed under the Accessto Information Act L'information divulguée en vertu de la loi sur l'accés à l’infonmation

s.19(1) IMPORTANT TO NOTE: THE MAJORITY OF PC'S FROM INDIA AND PAKISTAN ARE NOT CLEAR.

The PC below states the applicant is/had been residing at the above address. He has not been found involved in any criminal offences as per Crime Records Officer (CRO) and concerned Police Station record till date. The PC does not indicate that the no record was based on the address or time specified. Therefore, PC is acceptable.

If the PC indicates that the individual has resided at a specific address for a certain length of time but it's unclear if the entire time frame that you need the PC for is covered for criminality, the Program Assistant must accept the PC.

For example if the PC states: “This is to certify that Client's name, who has been residing at 123 Anywhere Street, New Delhi, India from May 5, 2013 to May 10, 2015, has no criminal record"

In this instance it's not clear if there is no record during that time frame or no record ever, so the Program Assistant must err on the side of caution and give the client the benefit of the doubt and accept the PC at the program assistant level. If you are unsure if a PC from India or Pakistan is acceptable, consult the RA.

Below are examples of specific countries where PCs may have particular remarks requiring additional interpretation or require different procedures to obtain:

Philippines PC issued by: National Bureau of Investigation (NBI) Remarks: No Record on File-OK No Derogatory Record-OK No Criminal Record-Further review required No Pending Case-Further review required Record as Stated-Further review required

United Kingdom (England, Wales, Scotland, Northern Ireland, Channel Islands) PC information issued by: UK Association of Chief Police Officers (ACPO) PC Issued by: National Police Chiefs Council (NPCC) Remarks: No Trace-OK No Live Trace-Further review required Cautions-Further review required Impending Prosecution-Further review required Positive-Further review required Rehabilitation of Offenders Act-Further review required

All EE Eligibility notes should list England, Wales, Scotland, Northern Ireland and Channel Islands as “United Kingdom” as the country for the PC

Saudi Arabia There are some issues with obtaining police certificates from Saudi Arabia for non-Saudi Arabian nationals. Refer to IRCC website for detials.

If the client is not a permanent resident of Saudi Arabia they are unable to obtain a PC. Add the following in the EE Eligibility note:Previous Residence inSaudi Arabia

There are other countries that only issue PCs to residents, they should be noted similarly.

Singapore

## IRCC requires a Certificate of Clearance (COC) in order to assess an applicant's admissibility to

Canada and will not accept a Statutory Declaration of no criminal conviction.

# Israel

## Effective January 5, 2022:

We no longer require a copy of the Israeli PC to be uploaded into the application/UCI in GCMS. We can accept the note example below, and similar notes to the sample.

If you are unsure of the acceptability of the note in GCMS, email the RA for review. If you have concerns and would like a copy of the PC, do NOT email the email address in the note in GCMS. Instead, email the RA or see TL for further direction.

Sample GCMS Note the Tel Aviv Office enters:

### Israeli PC's

The Embassy of Canada to Israel in Tel Aviv (IRCC Tel Aviv') confirms the receipt of an original Israeli police certificate for this applicant. There are NO criminal convictions listed on the police certificate. The Israeli Police directly sends these certificates to IRCC Tel Aviv and this entry into GCMS notes should be considered as having met this particular admissibility requirement. Do note that Israeli authorities expunge convictions in a way that is not equivalent to Canadian pardons/suspensions. In case of doubts regarding an applicant's criminal history where they get a blank police certificate but do declare criminality. a court transcript, foreign statutes and translation should be requested directly from them.

Principal offices which need the certificate uploaded into the application must email (tavivim@international.gc.ca) to do so after having set Tel Aviv as secondary office in GCMS. -

L'Ambassade du Canada en Israël à Tel Aviv ("IRCC Tel Aviv") confirme avoir reçu un extrait de casier judiciaire original pour cette personne. L'extrait reçu ne contient AUCUNE condamnation criminelle. La Police israélienne envoie ces certificats directement à IRCC Tel Aviv et cette note SMGC peut être acceptée preuve que la personne s'est conformée à cette exigence précise dans le contexte de l'évaluation de son admissibilité. Veuillez noter que les autorités israéliennes effacent/pardonnent les condamnations selon une méthode qui n'est pas équivalente aux pardons et suspensions canadiennes. En cas de doute au sujet du casier judiciaire d’une personne qui obtient un extrait vierge mais qui déclare des condamnations. veuillez demander à la personne de fournir une transcription la décision du tribunal et du code criminel local ainsi que leur traduction.

Les bureaux principaux qui requièrent une copie numérisée du certificat peuvent contacter IRCC Tel Aviv par courriel (tavivim@international.gc.ca) en prenant soin, au préalable, d'ajouter Tel Aviv comme bureau secondaire dans le SMGC.

Australia The Complete Disclosure version is not acceptable.

Qatar Some Police certificates from Qatar do not cover the entire time residing in Qatar. Qatar does not provide certificates to clients while on visitor visas, only when they receive resident status, which explains the missing time.

- If a Qatar PC does not cover the entire time spent in that country; we will continue processing, note the PC as “Review Required” and change the EE Eligibility note to “Review Required” for the recommendation.
Malaysia Foreign nationals who have resided in Malaysia for less than one year are unable to obtain a police certificate from the Ministry of Foreign Affairs in Malaysia. On the How-To website, clients are instructed to submit a letter with their application stating “I cannot provide a police certificate for the time I spent in Malaysia because certificates are only available to people who have lived in Malaysia for one year or more." The Program Assistant must review the client's address and background history to confirm the time spent in Malaysia.

- If the time spent in Malaysia is less than one year, continue processing the application. Add the following to your EE Eligibility note BELOW the criminality recommendation line: Indicates residence in Malaysia for less than 1 year If the time spent in Malaysia is more than one year, send the client a request letter for the Malaysian police certificate and move the file forward. Client should be given 60 days to provide the police certificate
Hong Kong Hong Kong Police Certificates will be requested using the letter template EE-Hong Kong PC Request – EN (Or EE-Hong Kong PC request – FRENCH). No item line is required, as the PC is uploaded direct to IRCC from Hong Kong. These are processed by the Hong Kong office. In addition to the specific letter, Hong Kong must be added as a secondary office following the below steps:

In the PR screen once you’ve completed your file and the letter is sent Click the ellipsis under Secondary Office GCMS Fle Edt viewNavigateQuery Toole Holp OSte MapReport(s) Addresses PR P8

# Nepal

## Colombia

The How To site indicates: You don't need to provide a police certificate with your application. The client may provide documentation advising that they require a letter or that they have already requested the police certificate from Colombia. No Item line is required when requesting a Colombia PC. It is uploaded directly by Colombia.

Client provides a letter indicating they requested the PC: If the client provides a letter indicating that they have submitted a request to Colombia for their police certificate:

Park the file, and send an email to your Team Lead using the subject line “Colombia PC”. Hold the file till the TL advises if the PC is provided and ok to proceed or if it must be requested. If ok to proceed, note it as provided. Move the file forward

Client provides a letter indicating they need a request letter or screenshot of How To or nothing is provided:

Send a General Police Certificate request letter giving the client 60 days. The client will not receive a copy of the PC, it is provided directly to IRCC. Once the letter is sent the PC is noted as request letter sent and the file is moved forward as normal and noted as PC request letter sent.

Effective for files with an ITA date of November 15, 2023 for Colombia – refer to the How-To for the most recent instructions. ITA's received prior to that date – use the instructions above. Files with an ITA date AFTER November 15, 2023– the Colombia How-To instructs the applicant to provide the email from the service provider confirming they applied for the Background Verification Certificate. If the email confirming the PC has been requested is provided, mark the police certificate item line as review required and include the edoc number. The Background Verification Certificate should not be sent to the applicant but be sent to the IRCC visa office directly. If the client provides a police certificate from Colombia, refer to the RA for review. NOTE: If the client provides a Colombia PC and the program assistant is unsure of the acceptability, these are referred to the RA for review. Hold and add a parked note to the file till the RA advises how to proceed.

## OTHER ISSUES

Check the details of each country using the How To website, as requirements may vary. For example:

- Check Client and IMM notes in GCMS. Occasionally, the PC may be received at CIO and attached to the EE application. Ex: Israel PCs; not released to the client
- Some issue more than one type of clearance: National record check vs. Local police station records Some issue both fingerprint and name check based certificates Multiple certificates (national, state/provincial, police, and court records) are sometimes required. Ex: PCs from Croatia generally require a certificate issued by the Ministry of Justice and a certificate issued by the court, unless the applicant has lived outside of Croatia for the last six months or longer Some only issue PCs if the applicant provides a formal request letter from IRCC Some will not send PCs to the applicant, only to IRCC offices Some will issue PCs through the Consulate, High Commission, or Embassy offices Some will not issue PCs to non-citizens, non-residents, or only issue to a proxy appointed to apply in the country on the foreign national's behalf Some PCs do not always clearly indicate the outcome of an arrest or charge
- If the PC is released for a specific country, other than Canada, it is ok to accept it. This does not nullify the information regarding the criminal background check for the client In a case where the recommendation needs to be set as Review Required due to a pending PC and Stat Question or just a Stat Question, the EE Eligibility note should be edited. Ex: Recommendation:Review Required –Pending outstanding PC and Stat Question(s) orRecommendation: Review Required – Stat Question(s)

## POLICE CERTIFICATES NOT PROVIDED

GCMS provides multiple item lines to upload their police certificates labelled "Police Certificate”, the number of items lines GCMS provides to the client is based on residency information listed on the application. Using this residency information, GCMS will only be going back 10 years, or from the time the client turned 18 (whichever comes first) to determine which PCs the client is required to provide.

Due to the fact GCMSis asking for specific PCs, CIO WILL NOT reject applications that have missing PCs if the applicant wasn't prompted by GCMS.

Program Assistants will ONLY review the past 10 years, or from the time the applicant turned 18 (if it's less than 10 years). Program Assistants will not be required to review any residency information outside of the 10 year period and will not be requesting PCs outside of the 10 year period.

Note: PM03 officer discretion can still be used if the officers would like to see PCs that weren't

requested by GCMS (i.e., dating back more than 10 years or for countries with less than 6 months residency). Review Address History, Education/Personal History, and Travel History. Note in the ADMM which countries the client(s) have spent 6 months or more in a row. Then review incoming correspondence to see if the correct PCs are provided.

If a Program Assistant receives an application that is missing a required PC, please complete the following steps:

- Verify if GCMS prompted the client to submit the PC
- If GCMS did not prompt the client to submit the PC, DO NOT reject the application: Move the file forward – no need to request clarification for further information Criminality Recommendation should set to “Review Required" GCMS note should be added below the Criminality Recommendation
line: [Country] PC-not provided.

- If GCMS DID prompt the client for the REQUIRED PC but the REQUIRED PC was not provided, the program assistant will reject the application.

MANDATORY DOCUMENTS Information obtained December 19th 2017. Make note the PDI is updated regularly. For the most up-to-date information on mandatory documents; use the web link listed below.

## Translation of Documents

For PCs: If a certificate is in a language other than English or French, the client must provide IRCC with a copy of the original document as well as a translation completed by a certified translator.

For all other Documents: If a supporting document is in a language other than English or French, the applicant must provide IRCC with:

- the English or French translation; and
- an affidavit from the person who completed the translations; and
- a certified photocopy of the original document
The instruction guide for Express Entry candidates and applicants explains how to meet these requirements.

## MANDATORY DOCUMENTS FOR ALL APPLICANTS

The following documents are mandatory and must be provided by all applicants. Applications that do not contain the following documents will be rejected as incomplete, and fees will be refunded accordingly.

Medical Examination Confirmation

Program Assistant Tip: It is acceptable if a copy of the medical has not been uploaded in the applicant's correspondence but is viewable within the client's T-number.

Purpose: To assess the health of the applicant and their family members

## Document requirements

The medical examination confirmation is required up front and must be valid at the time of application. The applicant must include the immigration medical examination (IME) number in their e-APR. The applicant must provide a copy of either the information printout sheet or the Upfront Medical Report form [IMM 1017B, whichever the panel physician provides upon completion of the medical examination. Exceptions are made only in rare circumstances that are outside of the client's control (e.g., cancellation of the appointment by the panel physician at the last minute without time to reschedule).

## In exceptional circumstances, IRCC may accept

- proof that a medical appointment is scheduled; or
- a letter of explanation of best efforts.
However, these are not a guarantee of acceptance.

Individuals who must submit this documentation

- The principal applicant Their spouse or common-law partner
- Their dependent children, including non-accompanying

## Police Certificates

Purpose: To determine if the applicant or their family members have a criminal record or poses a security risk to Canada

Document requirements

According to the requirements below, the system will generate upload fields for the police certificates required in the document checklist using the personal history, travel history and address history provided by the applicant. However, it is always at an officer's discretion to request a new or additional police certificate from the applicant. Applicants may also provide additional police certificates in the letter of explanation field.

- For the applicant's current country of residence, the police certificate must have been issued no more than six months before the submission of the e-APR. For countries in which the applicant no longer resides, the police certificate must have been issued after the last time the applicant stayed in that country for 6 months or more in a row.
- Police certificates meeting the above requirements can be accepted even if they have an expiry date that has passed. This includes the requirement to provide a police certificate for countries in which the individual has travelled for 6 months or more in a row, whether or not the individual had an established residential address during this time.
- Police certificates are required upfront and are mandatory for each country (except Canada) where an individual has spent 6 months or more in a row within the last 10 years. The individual does not need to provide one for any period of time before the age of 18.
- Police certificates need to be a scan of the original police certificate(s) in colour. Certified true copies and unauthorized copies are unacceptable and will result in the application being rejected as incomplete.
Applications that do not include a required police certificate and do not provide supporting documentation where required will be rejected as incomplete.

The officer can exercise discretion when assessing whether police certificates that do not fall within standard IRCC parameters may still be required to process the application. When necessary, an officer can use discretion in their determination of whether or not a client is admissible to Canada by using other documents or mechanisms that are available to them.

Some countries will not issue police certificates to applicants, and instead will only communicate directly with the relevant Canadian authorities. In such cases, existing IRCC procedures to obtain documentation should be followed.

- proof of having requested a police certificate for such countries; and

upload the document in the country-specific field. The uploaded document must show that the applicant requested a police certificate as soon as possible after receiving their invitation to apply. In such exceptional cases, the client will be advised to upload a document into the upload field stating country-specific details that support their explanation of best efforts. Document requirements

- The personalized document checklist in applicants' online account prompts the applicant with an upload field for each work experience declared. At the completeness check, the processing office determines only whether the required documents are included. The validity or fraudulence of the information is assessed only if and when the application is placed into processing.

The following documents are mandatory for each work experience declared:

- A reference or experience letter from the employer, which should be an official document printed on company letterhead (must include the applicant's name, the company's contact information [address, telephone number and email address], and the name, title and signature of the immediate supervisor or personnel officer at the company), should indicate all positions held while employed at the company and must include the following details: job title, duties and responsibilities, job status (if current job), dates worked for the company, number of work hours per week and annual salary plus benefits; and
- If the applicant is self-employed, articles of incorporation or other evidence of business ownership, evidence of self-employment income and documentation from third-party individuals indicating the service provided along with payment details (self-declared main duties or affidavits are not acceptable proof of self-employed work experience).

If the work experience is in Canada, proof may include copies of T4 tax information slips and notices of assessment issued by the Canada Revenue Agency (the time period for these documents should reflect the work experience timeframe [e.g., work experience from 2006 to 2008 requires only documents from those calendar years]).

Individuals who must submit this documentation

- The principal applicant
- Their spouse or common-law partner (if work experience in Canada is claimed)

# Mandatory Documents (if applicable)

The following documents are mandatory if applicable (i.e., based on the personal circumstances of the applicant and their dependents). When applicable, applications that do not contain the following documents will be rejected as incomplete and fees will be refunded.

Copy of Birth Certificate or Other Equivalent Document Program Assistant Tip: Ensure the name of at least one parent is included on the child's birth certificate. Birth Certificates based on Passports are not acceptable.

Purpose: To prove the dependent children's relationship to the parents Document requirements

- This document is required only if the applicant identifies dependent children.
- The document must be one of the following: birth certificate; equivalent document from the birth country; or letter of explanation (where no birth certificate is available in the country)
Individuals who must submit this documentation

- All the dependent children, including non-accompanying
### Proof of Studies

Program Assistant Tip: ONLY mandatory for FSWor PNP-FSW LOB for the PA only

Purpose: To confirm education claims and validate that the applicant meets program requirements Document requirements

- To receive program-specific points for education, the applicant must either provide proof of having completed a Canadian secondary or post-secondary
educational program; or obtain an Educational Credential Assessment (ECA) and provide the ECA reference number in their application, along with proof of the foreign educational credentials for which points are being claimed.

- Proof of completed Canadian or foreign educational credentials can include copies of secondary or post-secondary education documents (certificates, diplomas or
degrees); and 0transcripts for successfully completed secondary or post-secondary studies, if available.

- Applicants must provide the Designated Learning Institution (DLI) number of the Canadian Institution where they completed their highest degree to be assigned points for their Canadian educational credentials. At the EE profile and application stage, the system will ask the candidate to enter
the # of their DLI by using the list of DLI posted on IRCC website. The system will insert a note if the number of the DLl doesn't match for the officer to review

- In cases where an applicant's background information suggests that a Canadian education credential may have been obtained via distance learning either abroad or while present in Canada, it is at the officer's discretion to request for supporting documentation. If it is determined that the applicant obtained more than 50 percent of their
education via distance learning, the applicant should not be awarded additional

## Comprehensive Ranking System (CRS) points for having a Canadian education

credential. If the reduction in CRS points results in the applicant having a score below the minimum CRS, the application should be refused per A11.2.

- Where an ECA has been obtained, the applicant must provide the ECA reference number in their e-APR for online validation directly from the issuing organization. A scanned copy of the ECA is not required when submitting the e-APR but may
be requested by IRCC at a later date.

## Applicants are instructed to indicate that their education was completed by

correspondence by typing in brackets {by correspondence} on the same line as the education institution name found in the Education Background Information tab of GCMS. The discretion remains with the officer to decide if a police certificate is required for the country of the education institution

### Individuals who must submit this documentation

- The principal applicant
- Their spouse or common-law partner

### Letter of Offer from the Employer

Program Assistant Tip: NOT mandatory for CEC, FSW, PNP-CEC, or PNP-FSW LOBs.

### A Letter of Offer may be required for FSTor PNP-FST applications.

Purpose: To confirm an applicant's qualifying offer of arranged employment, to validate that

## the applicant meets program requirements and to screen for concerns of fraud

#### Document requirements

- This document is required only if the applicant claims to have a qualifying offer of arranged employment.
- A letter from the employer(s) offering the job in Canada is required, and it must 0be printed on company letterhead and include the applicant's name, the
company's contact information (address, telephone number and email address), and the name, title and signature of the applicant's proposed immediate

### supervisor or personnel officer; and

oinclude the following details: expected start date, commitment that the applicant will be employed on a continuous, paid, full-time work, for work that is for at least one year after issuance of a permanent resident visa, job title, duties

## and responsibilities, current job status (if current job), number of work hours per

week and annual salary plus benefits.

## Marriage Certificate

Program Assistant Tip: A marriagecertificate is required. Not required if married to a Canadian/Permanent resident. Purpose: To validate marital status claims

## Document requirements

- This document is requested only when marital status is listed as married.
- The marriage must be valid under the law of the country where it took place and under Canadian law. A marriage performed in an embassy or consulate must comply with the law of
the host country where it took place, not the country of nationality of the embassy or consulate.

- If the applicant married in Canada, a copy of the marriage certificate issued by the province or territory where the marriage took place must be provided.
- If the applicant married outside Canada, a copy of a legal certificate issued by the country, the territory or the region where the marriage took place must be provided.
## Individuals who must submit this documentation

- The principal applicant
## Divorce Certificate and Legal Separation Agreement

Program Assistant Tip: A divorce certificate IS required. Not required if divorced from a Canadian/ Permanent resident.

## Legal Separation Agreement documents ARE required. Not required if separated from a Canadian/ Permanent resident.

## Purpose: To validate marital status claims

### Document requirements

- The divorce certificate is requested only when marital status is listed as divorced.
- If the applicant divorced in Canada, a copy of the divorce order or certificate of divorce issued by the province or territory where the divorce took place must be provided.
- If the applicant divorced outside Canada, a copy of a legal certificate issued by the country, the territory or the region where the divorce took place must be provided.
- A copy of the legal separation agreement must be provided, if available.
## Individuals who must submit this documentation

- The principal applicant
- Their spouse or common-law partner

- A copy of a death certificate, executorships or other legal certificate issued by the country, the territory or the region where the death occurred must be provided.
Individuals who must submit this documentation

- The principal applicant

- This document is requested only when the dependent child status is listed as adopted.
- A copy of the adoption certificate, legal adoption papers, and/or other relevant documents issued by the country, the territory or the region that formalized the adoption must be provided.

- The dependent children, including non-accompanying

## Other documents

As applicable to their situation, applicants are required to submit the following documents through their online account as part of their e-APR. The system does not let applicants submit their e-APR until documents have been uploaded into the provided fields.

In contrast to other documents, however, applications will not be rejected as “incomplete" if the documents listed below are not provided to the proper specification.

Applications can, however, be refused if the processing office does not have enough information on file to be satisfied that the applicant meets the requirements of the program to which they are applying.

Proof of relationship to a relative in Canada, status of a relative in Canada and residence status of a relative in Canada (including a brother or sister for the purpose of obtaining additional CRS points)

Purpose: To validate a claim of, and award points associated with, having

- a relative in Canada who is a Canadian citizen or permanent resident (for the Federal Skilled Worker Program adaptability points); an adult brother or sister who is a Canadian citizen or permanent resident residing in Canada (for additional CRS points).
## Document requirements

- This documentation is required if the applicant indicates that they, or their accompanying spouse or common-law partner, have a family member in Canada who is a permanent resident or a Canadian citizen. •A copy of both sides of the family member's Canadian citizenship document, Canadian birth certificate and/or permanent resident card must be provided. Evidence that the family member currently resides in Canada must be provided (a residential lease, mortgage documents, utility bills, etc.). The documents must show their address in Canada and should be recent (dated within six months prior to submission).
- Proof of the relationship of the applicant or the accompanying spouse or common-law partner to the family member must also be provided (e.g., a birth certificate, an official document naming the applicant as a relative, a copy of the inside back cover of the relative's passport showing the relative's parents' marriage certificate, legal adoption documents and any other documents that prove or describe the relationship).

Authority to Release Personal Information to a Designated Individual form

# [IMM 5475]

Program Assistant Tip: IMM5475 must be requested if the client lists a Designated Individual in the Notes tab, if not otherwise provided, after promotion.

Purpose: To appoint a designated individual to inquire about the status of an application and/or change the applicant's address information

Document requirements

- This document is not requested by the system, even if the applicant has indicated they are designating an individual.
- This document should be provided in the open field (see below) if the applicant has indicated they would like to designate an individual.
- The IMM 5475 form (PDF, 593.57 KB) must be signed by the applicant and, as applicable, their spouse or common-law partner if they also wish to release personal information to the same designated individual.
- Dependent children 18 years of age or older must complete their own form if they wish to authorize the release of their information to a designated individual.
Individuals who must submit this documentation

- Their spouse or common-law partner, if applicable
- Their accompanying dependent children 18 years of age or older
Documents related to the e-APR not captured in other fields

Purpose: To allow the applicant to provide IRCC with any other information they feel is relevant to their application

## Document requirements

- Submission of this documentation is at the applicant's discretion. One upload field is provided for each of the applicant, their spouse or common-law partner and their dependent children, as applicable.
- Multiple documents must be compiled and uploaded as a single file. This should be used to provide documentation to support a declaration of status as “legally separated” (e.g., a letter of explanation, legal documents, etc.), if applicable.
- This should be used to submit the Authority to Release Personal Information to a Designated Individual form [IMM 5475 (PDF. 593.57 KB)], if applicable.

## APPENDIX J

## ECA AND CANADIAN EDUCATION VERIFICATION

As per the IRCC website: [http://www.cic.gc.ca/english/immigrate/skilled/assessment.asp](http://www.cic.gc.ca/english/immigrate/skilled/assessment.asp) We will only accept an ECA from one of the organizations designated by Citizenship and Immigration Canada (CIC) (below).

## The original ECA report must:

- be issued on or after the date CIC designated the organization,
- not be more than five years old on the date that CiC gets a) your Express Entry profile, and b) your application for permanent residence, and
- show your credential(s) is equal to a completed Canadian one
Note: You will have to provide the reference number from the ECA report in your Express Entry

profile, as well as in your application for permanent residence. If we cannot validate the number you enter, you will need to provide an electronic copy to confirm. Keep a copy in case we need to see it.

- Verify the ECA report on the issuing agency website based on the information entered by the client in GCMS. This information is found under the EE Eligibility tab, Education subtab. Refer only to records with a status of “Received”. The ECA report information number and issuing agency should be provided, however if the applicant did not enter this information in the EE Eligibility tab a copy of the ECA uploaded within the client's incoming correspondence can be used All education documents must be reviewed for the PA and the Spouse/CL. The following example shows an ECA report verified by WES. ECAs and websites for
other designated organizations will vary.

# ECA NOT AVAILABLE ON WEBSITE

Please note additional information for ECA Instructions and High School diplomas.

Effective Immediately – Changes to ECA instructions for Express Entry

The guidelines below will be applied to all Express Entry lines of business – FSW, FST, CEC and PNP. Guidelines apply to both the PA & the SP. In other words, if a PA or SP have an ECA that cannot be verified on the application, the application will be rejected. Please see SITUATIONs section below and steps to take:

Note: When Reviewing Education on an Application

# ** Along with reviewing Incoming Correspondence, please review the Received lines for

Education under EE Eligibility tab > Education sub tab.

SITUATIONS:

1.If the ECA assessment is pendingor on hold as of the received date, then the application will be held for all Express Entry lines of business – FSW, FST, CEC and PNP.Refer to the TL for review via email. Template is located in Appendix GG.
2. If the ECA is expired as of the received date-rejection
3. (a)ECA (WES only) – ECAs from WES must indicate for immigration purposes.
If WES is not for immigration purposes – Reject. If the WES document cannot be verified-Request a copy if one was not provided. If the WES document is for immigration purposes and the copy cannot be verified-Send an email to the RA Inbox.

(b)All other ECAs– If you are able to verify – ok to continue. If the ECA cannot be verified-Request a copy if one was not provided. If provided copy cannot be verified = Refer to the RA.
4. If the client has provided more than one education document type (i.e. Canadian Education and an ECA), but you are not able to verify one (or more) of the documents, refer to the ECA and Canadian Education Guidelines in this Appendix to determine if it must go to the RA or can be processed.
5. CANADIAN HIGH SCHOOL (SECONDARY EDUCATION) Although a Canadian High School diploma may be indicated and/or provided on an Express Entry application, it is not required and the absence of this document from the application is NOT considered a reason for rejection. Exception: If a Canadian high

## ECA AND CANADIAN EDUCATION GUIDELINES

** If a copy of the ECA was not provided, this must be requested prior to any further action** **Canadian Credentials must be verified for the Institution's legal status (Registered, Authorized or Recognized) and noted using the CiCIC site. * There HAs to be a received line for the education under EE Eligibility for us to be able to reject

*** NOTE: If a client provides foreign education under the Canadian education line (Pulls in the ADMM as Canadian) (thus was not prompted for an ECA) do not reject. Request a copy of the ECA if not provided. You can proceed if the client provides the ECA.

1Both Canadian credential provided and verifiable AND ECA provided but is not verifiable:

a. If Canadian Education is highest = note Canadian Education and add the following note for the ECA "unable to verify ECA."Note foreign credentials are provided or not, move forward.
b. If ECA the highest and unable to verify (not for immigration purposes, expired) = rejection
C. If ECA on hold or in progress-place the file on hold and email TL (Template found in Appendix GG.) Hold till advised to proceed.
d. If unable to determine which is the highest level of education = send to the RA with subject line: Highest Level of Education
2. Both ECA provided and verifiable AND Canadian credential provided under incoming correspondence:
a.If Canadian Education is verifiable (see #4 a or b.) = note and move forward
b. If Canadian Education is not verifiable (see #4 c, d, or e) = send to RA
3.Only Foreign education with ECA
a.If ECA verifiable = note and move forward
b.If ECA not able to verify (not for immigration purposes, expired) = reject
C.If ECA provided is for immigration purposes but you are unable to verify = send verification email as per R10 manual
d. If ECA on hold or in progress-place the file on hold and email TL (Template found in Appendix GG.) Hold till advised to proceed.
集。 Only Canadian Education indicated under EE Eligibility received line:

a. If transcripts and diploma/certificate are provided = note and move forward
b.If only diploma/certificate provided = note and move forward
C.If only transcripts provided = send to RA
### d. If letter from university and transcripts provided = send to RA

e. If letter from University provided and no transcripts = send to RA
f.If FSW or PNP-FSW and no education is provided (for PA only) = rejection
5.If no ECA is indicated under EE Eligibility received line = ECA not required, note if foreign credentials are provided or not and move forward
a. Exception for PA only: applying under FSW or PNP (FSW) and does not have a Canadian credential = rejection
6.If no ECA is indicated under EE Eligibility received line but client provides ECA under incoming correspondence
a. If ECA verifiable = note and move forward
b. If ECA not verifiable (not for immigration purposes, on hold, in progress, expired) = enter "Foreign Credentials Provided" or "Foreign Credentials Not Provided" in note and move forward.
C. Exception for PA only: applying under FSW or PNP (FSW) and does not have a Canadian credential = rejection
7. If the PA's NOC code is either 31100, 31101 or 31102 the ECA MUST be issued by MCC for all LOB
a. ECA issued by MCC = note and move forward
b. ECA issued by any other provider and no MCC = rejection
C. MCC provided and verifiable and ECA provided but not verifiable = note and move forward
d. No MCC ECA # provided (ECA # field is blank and foreign credential provided) = send to RA

For any ECA issues or discrepancies in the results, email the RA inbox, IRCC.CIOResourceAgent-BRCDSAgentdeRessources.IRCC@cic.gc.ca The designated individual for the office will contact the agency to request an update/confirmation as per your office procedures.

Provide the following information in your email request:

# ECA Agency

ECA Reference # ECA date of issue Applicant Name Applicant DOB Application #

The ECA agency will advise our office of the outcome of this update/confirmation. The designated individual will forward this information to the Program Assistant. Refer to the RA if necessary on how to proceed if the information is not provided to you by the designated individual from the office.

Occasionally, an ECA is not available on the agency's website for verification or if there are discrepancies in the results. For any ECA issues or discrepancies in the results, email the RA inbox, IRCC.CIOResourceAgent-BRCDSAgentdeRessources.IRCC@cic.gc.ca The designated individual will contact the agency to request an update/confirmation as per your office procedures. Provide the following information in your email request:

# ECA Agency

ECA Reference # ECA date of issue Applicant Name Applicant DOB Application #

The agency will advise our office of the outcome of this update/confirmation. The Resource Agent will send a request letter to the client requesting the release of this information – giving the client 30 days and advise the program assistant when the ECA is available and the program assistant can proceed.

If the client has provided more than one education document type (i.e. Canadian Education and anECA), but you are not able to verify one (or more) of the documents, send the application number and reason to the RA for review and guidance (use subject line-Education Review). The RA will assess the file and advise how to proceed.

## HOW TO VERIFY CANADIAN EDUCATION

The client may provide proof of completed Canadian Education (ex. transcripts, a copy of a diploma, certificate, a letter from the university, etc.) If only a letter or transcript is provided with no accompanying diploma or certificate please refer to the RA for review.

Verify that the Institution's legal status is Registered, Authorized, or Recognized using the online verification site.

[https://www.cicic.ca/868/Search-the-Directory-of-Educational-Institutions-in-Canada/index.canada](https://www.cicic.ca/868/Search-the-Directory-of-Educational-Institutions-in-Canada/index.canada) cicic..ca INDMIDUAL ASEESSOR EDLICATIONMOBILITY Find an educational institution

and Citizenship Canadaet Citoyenneté Canada Information disclosed under the Accessto Information Act L'information divulguée en vertu de la loi sur l'accés à l’infonmation

## HOW TO NOTE CANADIAN EDUCATION

- ALL CANADIAN EDUCATION MUST BE NOTED, EVEN IF THERE'S A HIGHER LEVEL PROVIDED (ex: Certificate AND Bachelor's degree, both are noted) If the client provided proof of a completed Canadian degree, (Bachelors, Masters, Doctorate) the education should be listed as “Bachelor's Degree, Master's Degree, Doctorate” (whichever is applicable) in your EE Eligibility note
- If the client provided proof of a completed Canadian certificate, diploma, or associate degree the Program Assistant will attempt to determine the length of the program and include this information in the EE Eligibility note. Examples of note: Certificate (1 year) or 2 Year Diploma. Occasionally the certificate or diploma provided by the client will indicate the
length of the program on it 0Other times the client will provide transcripts or a letter from the institution to accompany the certificate or diploma and they may be used to determine the length of the program oIf the length of the program is unclear, the Program Assistant should note Education as Review Required in correspondence and indicate “undeterminable edoc: #” in their EE Eligibility note

- When CICIC website indicated the institution is recognized, the notes will indicate Canadian institution is “verified”
- When CICIC website indicates the institution is authorized, notes will include statement “education institution authorized for certain programs; review required”.
- When CICIC website indicates the institution is registered, notes will include statement “unable to verify Canadian institution; review required". When you search an institution on the CICIC website and the legal status is blank, we will treat them as a registered status and your note will include the statement “unable to verify Canadian institution; review required".
EQUIVALENCY THAT IS NOT CANADIAN

- If the client provided an ECA report that shows an equivalency for any country other than a Canadian equivalency this is considered to be not acceptable and the file is to be rejected.

s.19(1) There are various ways to potentially locate an old passport number if the client didn't provide it. Some current passports have references to the previous passport number. Examples would be: India-the last page refers to the previous passport number. If it is blank, this indicates there was no previous passport; China – the previous number is usually listed on the second page, if provided by the client; Bangladesh (among other countries) – the previous ppt is listed on the BioData page. Each country is different in what is provided.
Once you have determined that this is your clients UCl either by finding a match under FOSS or with the oldest GCMS record, you now have to determine that the UCl does not have an incorrect client associated with it. ■ To do this, you must click the hyperlink into the UCl. # APPENDIX L VERIFYING AND ATTACHING MEDICALS TO UCI

# VERIFYING MEDICALS

### The client's upfront medical is uploaded in the client's Incoming Correspondence. All

### medicals should be opened, verified, and the UMI/IME/UF number noted prior to attaching

### to a UCl. Once this is complete, along with the proper search and promotion procedures

### (see Appendix K), follow the instructions below to properly attach the medical to the UCl.

Each client provides an upfront Medical, or a Medical Information sheet. The client's upfront medical information is often located within the client's T-number, or within a client's existing UCI. If the medical information is found within an existing UCI this means the medical has already been attached to that UCl, verify that the medical information is

### correct prior to returning to the application.

## APPENDIX M

## RCMP SCREENING/CLEARANCE

If the client has additional names not included on the application they must be entered in the Client's Name tab within the UCI, prior to startingRCMP Screening.

ONLY Initiate RCMP screening for any spouse/dependents on file 18 years of age and older who have been in Canada for 6 months or more who does not require Biometrics (i.e. non-accompanying) and has not provided an RCMP Police Certificate up front.

RCMP Screenings are to be initiated for NON ACCOMPANYING Spouse/CL and Dep 18+, between the ages of 18 and 65 who have resided in Canada for at least 6 months or more in a row.

A client may submit an RCMP Criminal Record Check with their application. Acceptable RCMP Criminal Record Checks contain the client's fingerprints and their photo (see example below). RCMP Criminal Record Checks must be issued within 6 months prior to the application Rec'd Date in order to be noted. Any Canada RCMP Criminal Record Checks issued prior to that date are not noted.

Example of a valid RCMP Criminal Record Check:

2A-2024-64537-000142

If an RCMP Screening must be initiated in GCMS, complete the following steps:

- On the PR Screen, Admissibilities tab, Criminality subtab
- If no client(s) are listed, complete the following steps; 0Click New Click the ellipsis under the UCI column, the Client Pick Applet window will appear Click on the client you wish to create a criminality line for and click OK then
proceed

- If client(s) are listed, complete the following step; Highlight the person you would like to submit a RCMP Screening for
- Under the Sub Activities screen Click New
Criminakty

Criminality ぐ 26oe Scarted

Click the dropdown arrow under Type field. Select RCMP Screening Click the dropdown arrow under Status field. Change from Not Started to Submitted Repeat these steps for each person on the file that requires an RCMP Screening Press CTRL+S Status must show either “Fingerprints Required” or “No Reportable Trace” prior to moving forward (click Alt+Enter to refresh)

If the person you are running a RCMP Screening for has a name that is more than 24 characters in length you will need to request RCMP Fingerprints for that client. Change the status from Retransmission Required to Submitted to Fingerprints Requested. Send a request for RCMP Fingerprints (see Appendix U).

If RCMP status generates to Fingerprints Required, RCMP Fingerprints will have to be requested (see Appendix U).RCMP status MUST be changed to "Fingerprints Requested” after request letter is sent.

- If an application includes a change of name, the Program Assistant must:
Verify if the name is included under the Names tab If not included, add the name as indicated on the document and select either Former or AKA as the Name Type

As Program Assistants, we are only adding AKA names under the following situations:

If there is an alternate name provided on the following documents:

Legal Change of Name Document Marriage Certificate Birth Certificate If we receive a letter from the client and it specifically says they go by another name (affidavit, or client information) If the ECA clearly indicates that different name/names were provided (we will not go looking for it otherwise on the individual education documents)

If an alternate name was provided but not listed on the above mentioned documents it does not have to be entered as an AKA in GCMS after promotion at this time. (ex: on diplomas)

PASSPORTS

In the instances where you have a passport with an unusual naming situation you should still be accessing the Naming Policy website for guidance on how to enter alternate names from the passport as AKAs as found in the R10 manual (Appendix O and Appendix N). This covers items like parents names on passports in the VIZ section, truncated names, patronymics and matronymics,
accented letters, and punctuation, etc....

ADDING FEES FROM PROVIDED RECEIPTS

Occasionally the client will provide additional fees with their incoming correspondence such as Biometrics Fees or the Right of Permanent Residence Fee (RPRF). To add the fees to the application complete the following steps: To allocate fees:

Go to fees tab Under payments click NEW Add receipt number under the IPRMS Receipt # Hit Tab and the rest of the information will populate, click alt+enter to refresh and a payment number will appear Scroll down to the Fees section and ensure there is a line available to allocate the fees to. If there is a line for the fees to allocate to, scroll up to the payment section and click Allocate. If there is no line under the fees section, create one by clicking new, and selecting the applicable fee line Once the fee line is generated, scroll up to the Payment section and click Allocate. The fees will automatically allocate to the payment line that was outstanding. Re-review fees to ensure they're accurate before proceeding

# APPENDIX U REQUEST LETTER

## French Correspondence/Request letters MusT be sent by the designated Bilingual Agent.

See Appendix V.

Before creating a letter ensure Author Fix is completed. See Appendix T

If the request is for a language test, Schedule A for gaps, etc. Allow the client 7 days to

### provide the document(s). If not provided after the allotted time frame, reject the application

as incomplete.

Once the language test and/or Schedule A for gaps, etc. has been received move the file forward.

NOTE: For a Schedule A that has been received, it must have the signature of the client. An electronic signature is only acceptable when the client provides a Schedule A, which includes the electronic signature caveat as seen below.

SIGNATURE Authority to disclose personal information By submitting this form, you consent to the release to Canadian government authorities of ll records and information any gov ament authority, including police, judicial and state authotlcosvmnmn,asscona.Tsimnse to assistinevaluaingyour suitabilityforadmissiontoCanadaorremaininginCanadapursuanttoCanadianlegisiation. Declaration of applicant understand that misrepresentation is an offence under section 127 of the Immigration and Refugee Protection Act and may result in a finding of inadmissibility to Canada or removal from Canada Iagree that by typing my name, I am electronically signing my application.

Signature Date: YY-MM-DD

The link for the Schedule A should be included in the request letter when sending for a Schedule A, so the client receives the proper form with the correct signature box: [https://www.canada.ca/content/dam/ircc/migration/ircc/english/pdf/kits/forms/eimm5669e.pdf](https://www.canada.ca/content/dam/ircc/migration/ircc/english/pdf/kits/forms/eimm5669e.pdf)

If the request is for a representative or designated individual form, but it is not provided, Do

### NOT REJECT, continue processing if it is otherwise R10 complete. Ensure the EE Eligibility

note does not indicate that the client has a rep or designated individual.

If the request is for a RCMP Criminal Record Check and/or an official request letter for PCs and the application is otherwise R10 complete, send the request letter allowing 60 days to

### provide the RCMP/PC; move the file forward.

## EDITING AND SENDING A REQUEST LETTER

Note:Letter editing will vary depending on the situation and/or type of letter being edited.

If required see your RA for letter inserts not found in the manual

Verify client's information on the letter; Name, UCl, item being requested, etc. OLocate the correct blurb in the Request Letter Templates section of the manual and copy it. Highlight the blurb that pulled in from your item line and paste the correct blurb over it. Letters are not to be freehanded. There are specific approved blurbs to use found in this manual. 。 Ensure you leave the clients name and UCI when inserting the letter template. 0Leave the bullets so your letter is clean and professional looking.

- Add the due date (date entered in GCMS prior to generating the letter) to the line "Please upload these documents to your IRCC secure account by: DATE" Make sure "Please upload thesedocuments to your IRCC secure account by: DATE" is highlighted, centered, bolded and size 12 font or 14 font depending on the letter (if your signature only is pushed to a second page, edit the letter so it is all on one page). These editing settings are located with the Home tab of the word document. 日5-G05 File Home Insert References MailingsRevView Add-ins 0 Cut12 Aa 3 1

## Example of Letter After Editing

Immigration. Refugees Immigration, Réfugiés and Čitizenship Canada et Citoyenneté Canada

Date: August 11, 2022 UCI: Application:

Email:

Dear

This is in reference to your application for permanent residence in Canada. In order for us to continue the review of your application, we require the following documents: 7 ITEMS THAT WERE REQUESTED WILL BE LISTED HERE Please upload and submit these documents to your IRCC secure account by August 18, 2022. Please note: The above information/documents must be receivedwithin 7 days from the date of this correspondence. If this deadline is not met, your application will be assessed based on the information that was provided with your application and your application could be rejected for being incomplete.

FAILURE TO RESPOND TO THIS REQUEST MAY RESULT IN THE REJECTION OF YOUR APPLICATION, NO OTHER REMINDER WILL BE SENT TO YOU If the documents are not already in English or French, all documents must be accompanied by English or French translations by certified translators. When original documents are requested, you must upload the original documents to your IRCC secure account. We do not accept copies signed by notaries. For more information on permanent residence in Canada, please visit our web site at: [http://www.canada.ca/immigration](http://www.canada.ca/immigration). Thank you for the interest you have shown in Canada.

# Sincerely,

To avoid attaching incorrect letters to the application, it is imperative that once the client's letter is attached to the application and sent, return to the documents folder and DELETE the PDF with the application number of the file. This MusT be done after every letter sent.

To access your Documents Folder: Locate the Folder Icon on the bottom of your screen. Click on the icon and a popup window will appear. On the left hand side of the popup window scroll until you locate Documents and click on it. Locate and select the document to be deleted. Press delete key Add a General Note under Notes tab indicating what was requested from the client. Park the application for items with 7 day or 30 day request time unless otherwise stated within the manual

After the client has submitted a requested document the document's Status field (in Incoming Correspondence) must be changed from Provided to Received and the Review Status field should be appropriately changed to either Checked or Review Required.

## REQUEST LETTERS FOR PCS

Some countries require an official request letter from CiC in order for the applicant to apply for a PC. Program Assistants will send request letters for these PCs if the application is otherwise R10 complete

- Both the General Police Certificate Request letter and the Request for documents letter may be used to request PCs. It is important to read ALL the information on the How To Website, this will determine which letter to send

o Some countries will release the PC directly to the client. For these countries the
Request for documents letter is sent (Template 2) Some countries only release the PC directly to IRCC. For these countries the General Police Certificate Request letter (Template 17), that includes CIO's mailing and courier addresses, must be sent

- Complete request letter steps 1-6. For step 7, create a Police Certificate item line if required. In the comments field, indicate the specific country (ex: Please provide a police certificate for Hong Kong) Complete remaining request letter steps and move the file forward

## PC REQUEST LETTER GUIDE

Police Certificate Request when Best Efforts Met – (60 days):

Use Template 2: Request for documents. Item line: Police Certificate Request letter blurb:

Police Certificate: You have provided proof that you applied for your(insert country name)police certificate. To avoid further delays in processing of your application, please upload the(name of certificate from How To)to your IRCC secure account upon receipt.

RCMP (60 days) – Fingerprints Required: (for non-accompanying Spouse or 18+Dep that spent 6 months or more in Canada; RCMP screening results indicate Fingerprints Required, change to Fingerprints Requested and request)

Use Template 2: Request for documents. Item line: RCMP Criminal Record Check Request letter blurb:

RCMP Criminal Record Check: Please provide an RCMP Certified Criminal Record Check based on fingerprints. Please check the CIC website (How to obtain a police certificate-Canada) and refer to the criminal record check link. Complete all steps as instructed on the RCMP website. You will find the link below.

[http://www.rcmp-grc.gc.ca/cr-cj/fing-empr2-eng.htm](http://www.rcmp-grc.gc.ca/cr-cj/fing-empr2-eng.htm)

If requesting FBI Clearances or fingerprints for RCMP checks, use “FBI Clearance” and “RCMP Criminal Record Check” as the item lines.

Reminder: Use the FBI and RCMP letter inserts in the corresponding request letter (for RCMP See Editing a RCMP Letter within this appendix)

For FBI requests use the following: You have provided proof that you applied for your USA FBI police certificate. To avoid further delays in processing of your application, please upload the FBI Certificate to your IRCC secure account upon receipt.

Common PC Request Countries and Templates to Use:

Note: these are not the only request countries for PCs – refer to the HowTo for specific country

information: [https://www.canada.ca/en/immigration-refugees-citizenship/services/application/medical-police/police-certificates/how.html](https://www.canada.ca/en/immigration-refugees-citizenship/services/application/medical-police/police-certificates/how.html)

Singapore: Item line: Police Certificate – Use Template 16 *if PC provided & doesn't cover full time, request new one using the provided blurb under Request Letter Templates. Colombia: No item line – Use Template 17. *if client indicates they already applied for Colombia PC-send for TL review (email subject line: Colombia PC) Hong Kong: No item line – Specific HK PC request letter template in letter kit. *add HK as secondary office, steps in Appendix F Fiji: Item line: Police Certificate. If COR is Fiji – Use Template 28. If COR is not Fiji – Use Template 29. Japan: Item line: Police Certificate – Use Template 2. Netherlands: Item line: Police Certificate. – Use Template 2. Mexico: If COR is Mexico: item line: Police Certificate – Use Template 2. If COR not Mexico, no item line – Use Template 17. ** If client indicates that Mexico PC has already been applied for:Refer to the TL so they can check to see if PC was provided and if acceptable/how to note it (email subject line: Mexico PC)

# ***If Mexico PC is provided & current COR is NOT Mexico:

We cannot accept Mexico police certificates provided directly by clients from the embassy or consulate for clients that are not currently residing in Mexico; a new Mexico PC must be requested so it can come directly to IRCC and not to the client. For new Mexico PC requests for those that were provided up front: Use Template 30 - Mexico Police Certificate Request-EN.

For clients whose COR is Mexico: there is no change in the process. Make sure you review the How-To website for the applicable guidelines.
