There are several Countries on the How To website with the following: “You do not have to provide a police certificate from (Country) unless we ask for one." If the PA, Spouse or DEP 18+ has been to one of these Countries for a total of 6 months or more,we are to send a request

# letter for the PC and the Recommendation is Review Required.

SOME Countries that will not release PC to clients who are not citizens/residents or have lived there less than a year (always check the how-to for the most up to date information):

- Malaysia: If client there less than 1 year, under criminality recommendation: Indicates residence in Malaysia for less than 1 year
- Saudi Arabia: If client is not a PR of Saudi Arabia, under criminality recommendation: Previous Residence in Saudi Arabia

Qatar: Some Police certificates from Qatar do not cover the entire time residing in Qatar. Qatar does not provide certificates to clients while on visitor visas, only when they receive resident status, which explains the missing time. If a Qatar PC does not cover the entire time spent in that country; we will continue processing, note the PC as “Review Required” and change the EE Eligibility note to “Review Required” for the recommendation.

Benin: If client is not resident, under criminality recommendation: Previous residence in Benin
- Burma (Myanmar): If client does not currently reside there and cannot get a police certificate: Previous residence in Burma (Mayanmar)

##### FRENCH EE APPLICATIONS R10 PROCEDURES

STOP: Before you send a file to the French inbox, keep in mind the inbox is for translations and sending French letters. If you have an RA situation, the file must still go to the regular CIO Express Entry RA inbox first.

##### Complete French EE Application

- If the French application appears to meet R10, enter the GCMS complete note with the addition of the following words entered at the TOP of the note: Recommend R10 Complete The Program Assistant will assign the file to RC-C13 EXCEPTION: All CEC and FST should be assigned to RC-C04 regardless of COR
and Language as these applications will always be worked in Canada.

- A bilingual employee will review file for R10, review all French correspondence and add applicable GCMS notes and assign file accordingly

## APPENDIX BB

DEFINITION OF A DEPENDENT CHILD

Effective October 24, 2017, the definition of a “dependent child” is: A dependent child, in respect of a parent, is a child who:

a. has one of the following relationships with the parent:
i. is the biological child of the parent, if the child has not been adopted by a person other than the spouse or common-law partner of the parent ii.is the adopted child of the parent
b. is in one of the following situations of dependency:
i.is less than 22 years of age and is not a spouse or common-law partner ii.is 22 years of age or older, has depended substantially on the financial support of the parent since before the age of 22 and is unable to be financially self-supporting due to a physical or mental condition Former Definitions of a Dependent Child:
Between August 1, 2014, and October 23, 2017, the definition of "dependent child" was as follows: A dependent child, in respect of a parent, is a child who

a. has one of the following relationships with the parent:
i.is the biological child of the parent, if the child has not been adopted by a person other than the spouse or common-law partner of the parent ii.is the adopted child of the parent
b. is in one of the following situations of dependency:
i.is less than 19 years of age and is not a spouse or common-law partner ii. is 19 years of age or older and has depended substantially on the financial support of the parent since before the age of 19 and is unable to be financially self-supporting due to a physical or mental condition For additional information refer tothe following link:
[http://cicintranet.ci.gc.ca/connexion/tools-outils/perm/definitions-eng.aspx?letterNum=4](http://cicintranet.ci.gc.ca/connexion/tools-outils/perm/definitions-eng.aspx?letterNum=4)

## APPENDIX CC

## ADDING A DEPENDENT TO AN APPLICATION

Before adding a Dependent the RA MusT confirm that the dependent in question should be added to the application. The RA will verify what documents and/or fees must be requested and received There are two ways to receive a request to Add a Dependent/Spouse/Common-Law Partner (DEP/SP/CL).

R10 in Progress-The client submits a request to add a DEP/SP/CL via Client Enquiry. R10 Complete-File is forwarded to a Program Assistant via email from the team leader.

CLIENT REQUESTS TO ADD A CANADIAN DEPENDENT

- If the client submits a request to add a DEP/SP/CLP and the individual is a Canadian Citizen adding the dependent is not required, as long as there is proof of Citizenship provided (Birth Certificate, Passport, PR card). For PR status citizens, refer to the RA, park the file, and await direction prior to proceeding, PR status must be verified.
- Note the following for requests with confirmed Canadian citizens at the bottom of EE note: Dependent not associated to application as [spouse/common-law partner/child] is a [Canadian Citizen OR Permanent Resident of Canada]. Proof of status provided.
ADDING A DEPENDENT- R10 IN PROGRESS

- If the application in progress contains Client Information or a Client Enquiry that requests to add a DEP/SP/CLP, make note of the DEP/SP/CLP to be added, and continue to review the remaining documents for R10 completeness. If the file is R10 incomplete
Proceed with rejection (See Appendix X) If the file is R10 complete Promote as per R10 processing procedures, do not add EE Eligibility note Park the file Refer to the RA for review and await direction. Once confirmation is received, add the DEP/SP/CLP follow the steps under Adding a Dependent R10 Complete When the documentation is received, fees allocated, and file is now R10 complete proceed to add in the EE Eligibility note, include the added DEP/SP/CLP in the note, ensure criminality is noted appropriately (see Appendix F) Enter the following at the bottom of your EE Eligibility note: Spouse/CLP/Dep child added after ITA

APPENDIX DD LANGUAGE TEST AUTHENTICITY If it is believed the language test is not authentic, or you are unable to locate the language test on the appropriate website (and the copy has been provided) forward the test information to the RA General Mailbox using the template below. (If a copy has not been provided, request the language test and attempt online verification again before proceeding with the next steps). Subject line: Language Test Review-E00#

## Hello,

There appears to be a discrepancy in the following language test results. Application Number: Client Name: DOB: Language Test Type: Language Test #:

Please review. Thank you.

An inquiry regarding the language test will be sent to the testing agency and verified by the resource agents. Park the file and await guidance on how to proceed.

A similar process is to take place if the program assistant appears to have a Education Credential Assessment or a required document that is potentially not authentic. Park the file, email the RA General Mailbox and await guidance on how to proceed.

The following steps are only taking place after the file has been confirmed by the RA or TL that a document has been deemed not authentic. Program Assistants are encouraged to consult Resource Agent and/or Team Leader before referring case to Program Support Unit for review of suspected fraudulent document.

Please follow steps below when referring a case to PS for review of suspected document(s) that are not authentic:

1. Cancel Biometrics so no BIL is generated/sent to the client
2. Promote application (if not already done)
3. Add application note with “General" label: App referred to PS for review of eDoc #.

RESOURCE AGENT EMAIL TEMPLATES

Note: Ensure you’ve reviewed and followed all instructions in the R10 Manual prior to proceeding with

sending a request to the Resource Agent. All emails to the resource agent must include the application number within the subject line regardless of subject matter.

Subject lines of email requests MusT include the Application Number and clearly identify what the enquiry is regarding. This is helpful to the RA for organizing incoming requests. Some popular subject lines are:

BIOMETRICS CANCEL CRIMINALITY (Also marked as high importance) PNP Verification Request UCI Verification Household Request PR Status Review Request Withdrawal PC Question Fee Issue Rep NOT in Good Standing Undisclosed Rep Dep not included in application Cancel Info Sharing Cancel RCMP – Stuck in Batch Sent Cancel RCMP Screening Language Test Deceased Rep Education Review

There may be additional reasons other than what is listed above for sending an Email to the RA. Be as detailed as possible in the description for organizational purposes.

Include application number for all requests either in the body of the email or added to the subject line after the title.

PNP Verification request for the province – provide a reason for the request REJECTION– CANCEL CRIMINALITY: Program Assistants are unable to cancel Criminality for In Progress items prior to a rejection. When emailing the RA, use the subject lineCANCEL Criminality. This will prompt the RA to cancel the criminality for the rejection as soon as possible

# APPENDIX II

RECONSIDERATIONS

For all reconsideration requests, any information received after the decision has been made is not acceptable. Exceptions to this procedure may arise due to circumstances at the time. Each case should be examined in detail on an individual basis.

### Examples of Reconsideration Requests:

Example 1: You rejected the application for a missing FBI clearance. PA submits a reconsideration request advising proof the FBI clearance was applied for was provided with the original submission. When you review the file again, you realize you over looked the FBI clearance application and tracking info of application to FBI office in Clarksburg, West Virginia. This is considered best efforts and the file should have moved forward in processing, not rejected. Since an error was made in processing, you must reopen the file if R10 is passed. NOTE: If something else is missing and R10 is incomplete, file will not be reopened. Discuss with your RA prior to sending updated rejection letter.

Example 2: You rejected the application as the PC on file was issued before the client's last date of residence in that country. PA submits a reconsideration request with a newer version of the police certificate and advises they made a mistake in uploading and forgot to include the new version of the police certificate. Decision remains in this case, no error was made during processing on the department's behalf and PA submitted an application that was R10 incomplete.

In some cases, notification of a reconsideration request is received via email from CSU.

CSU Procedures:

Upload the email request to the application Receive the reconsideration request when they run the Provided Report. Notify the Program Assistant by email of the reconsideration request. Forward a template response to the client (Edit template to request applicant monitor their IRCC secure account for further information).

## RECONSIDERATION-APPLICATION RE-OPENED

If after review you decide the file will be re-opened, you must complete the R10 review to verify no other mandatory documents are missing.

If the file is R10 complete, and the fees HAVE been refunded (fee records show as Refund Complete; Finance has completed the refund):

- Assign the application to your GCMS code with a 7 day due date. Enter the following reconsideration note: Reconsideration request received at CiO. After a thorough review of the request and application, file has been reopened. Processing fees have been refunded. Notification sent to PA via the IRCC secure account along with a request to repay the processing fees. Re-open the file Click History tab In the App Status field, use the dropdown arrow to change Cancelled to Open In the App Status Reason field, use the dropdown arrow and select In Progress
- Send reconsideration letter and request fees Correspondence tab, Outgoing subtab Click New In the Document field, select Request Letter Scroll down to Item(s) Requested section, click New In the Item field, select Payment Receipt

- Generate reconsideration letter (1.2 TEMPLATE-EE-Reconsideration-R10 passed – Reopened), letter must be edited, export, send and proceed with file (see Appendix U)
## RECONSIDERATION – DECISION REMAINS

- Enter the following reconsideration note: Reconsideration request received at CIO. After a thorough review of the application, decision remains. (Include details and any e-doc to reference the reason). Notification sent to client via the IRCC secure account. Generate reconsideration letter (letter type should be “other" (19 TEMPLATE-EE-Reconsideration – decision remains – ENG version), letter must be edited, export and send letter to advise PA that the decision remains (see Appendix U) In the PR screen, remove code from the Assigned To field

### CHARGING FILES IN GCMS

RC-C14: GCMS Issues. Charge the application to this code when there is a GCMS issue. Example: unable to promote an application

RC-C13: French. Applications containing French Correspondence requiring additional review will be charged to this code. A bilingual Agent will review the correspondence and forward the application accordingly. See Appendix V

- RC-C04: Centralized Network ALL CEC/FST files are assigned to this code. For FSW/PNP assignment is based on if current COR is Canada or the USA, R10 complete application RC-C01: QA Code. To be used when applications are being reviewed by a mentor for quality assurance before being finalized

 Eligibility note is entered, and the file is moving forward as R10 complete.

oUnder the Admissibility-Criminality section, highlight the client requiring the Biometrics – RCMP.

If Biometric – RCMP line is NOT populated or the existing line was cancelled click 'New' to create a new line. Using the dropdown arrow under Type select Biometrics – RCMP. AFTER PROMOTION – REJECTIONS

If the Biometric– RCMP line under the Criminality Sub Activities has a Biometric #, email your TL before rejecting and wait for instructions on how to proceed.

If the file is a rejection, Biometrics should have been cancelled prior to promotion; Biometrics – RCMP lines in the Sub Activities under Admissibility – Criminality lines were changed to Cancelled prior to promotion, the BIL letter should NOT have sent.

Complete rejection as normal with addition to the following steps:

- Refund Biometric Fee in addition to the other fees as per the process outlined in Appendix X. If the BIL letter is sent in error for a rejection (ex: the program assistant did not select cancel for the Biometrics – RCMP line in Admissibilities, Criminality), or the file was held for any reason, the BIL letter was sent and becomes a rejection, the program assistant is to send an email to their team leader indicating the BIL letter was sent in error or sent due to holding the file, and provide the application number. Hold the file in your code. Do not refund fees until advised how to proceed. The Biometric fees may not be refundable if the client already had their Biometrics completed

## To allocate Biometric fees:

Go to fees tab Under payments click NEW Add receipt number under the IPRMS Receipt # Hit Tab and the rest of the information will populate, click alt+enter to refresh and a payment number will appear Scroll down to the Fees section and ensure there is a line available to allocate the fees to. If there is a line for the fees to allocate to, scroll up to the payment section and click Allocate. If there is no line under the fees section, create one by clicking new, and selecting either Biometric Fees ($85) or Biometric Family Rate ($170)

Once the fee line is generated, scroll up to the Payment section and click Allicate. You can now manually send the BIL letter now that the fees are on file. If there are issues sending the BIL or fees are not paid correctly, refer to RC – C16 and add a general note “referred for Biometrics Review" after your EE Eligibility note.

# Appendix LL

PASSPORTS AND TRAVEL DOCUMENTS

Clients are required to provide a valid passport or travel document for travel to Canada. A passport or travel document is considered valid if it's:

legal not expired, and accepted by us (Immigration, Refugees and Citizenship Canada) The following documents are not acceptable (by IRCC) as travel documents to Canada as they are considered unreliable and are NOT valid for travel to Canada:

Documents we don't accept (unreliable travel documents) The following documents are considered unreliable and are not valid for travel to Canada:

- Any Passport Issued by Somalia
- Non-machine readable passports issued by the Czech Republic
- Temporary passports issued by the Republic of South Africa
- Provisional passports issued by Venezuela
Please be advised, effective immediately if these unreliable documents are provided as travel documents on an application, the application is a REJECTiON, the file is incomplete as the applicant has not provided a valid passport/travel document. They are not accepted by IRCC. The Program Assistant will insert into the REJECTION Letter the following link to the public information page, so the applicant can see the acceptable/unacceptable documents. Public information page below: [https://www.canada.ca/en/immigration-refugees-citizenship/services/passport-travel-document.html](https://www.canada.ca/en/immigration-refugees-citizenship/services/passport-travel-document.html)

s.19(1)
## Passport for Aliens is not a Passport

Please see below an example of a Passport for Aliens from Switzerland. The Passport for Aliens is the equivalent to a Travel document, it is not a PASSPORT.

This type of document is issued by some countries to non-citizens residing within their borders, who are unable to obtain a passport from their own country of nationality.

If you receive this type of document in lieu of a Passport, consider it a travel document for a “stateless” individual.

## Venezuela passport

If you receive an application with a Venezuelan passport that is missing, expired or valid for less than 6 months, send an email to the RA with 'Venezuelan Passport" as the subject line.

DO NOT reject the application if the Venezuelan passport is missing, expired or valid for less than 6 months. If R10 complete (valid for less than 6 months), add your EE Eligibility note before referring to the RA. If Venezuelan passport is valid for more than 6 months, process as usual (no need to send to the RA).

- As of October 1, 2023, upfront medicals (UFM) are NO LONGER REQUIRED to be provided upfront for Express Entry! However, if a medical is provided there is a temporary public policy in place from October 6, 2022 to October 6, 2024 which exempts some In-Canada clients from providing a new Medical.

### PNP VERIFICATION FAQS

Express Entry PNP Verifications-Questions & Answers

1. Why do I need to search at the top search bar of the PNC Workbook main page? Searching for nomination using the top search bar of the PNC workbook main page will generate the list of all spreadsheets where your client's nomination could be listed. When you have a file with a nomination extension, you should find at least 2 spreadsheets (one spreadsheet listing the initial nomination and one spreadsheet listing the extension).
2. When and why are PNP Verification emails sent to the Resource Agents (RA)? PNP Verifications are sent to the RA in many cases:
- Information listed in the PNC details in GCMS does not match the nomination information listed on the spreadsheet. Our office is unable to determine which set of information is correct; therefore, the province must be contacted to confirm the correct nomination details Information on the spreadsheet is missing – some fields are blank. Human error happens, sometimes the provinces forget to enter some information Your client's nomination is listed as a Base nomination instead of Enhanced. All Express Entry nominations should be listed on the spreadsheet as Enhanced
3. What are PNC details and who enters this information? Provinces and territories (PTs) that operate a Provincial Nominee Program (PNP) can nominate candidates through the Express Entry pool. Nunavut and Quebec do not operate a Provincial Nominee Program. When a PT is ready to nominate a candidate, they search the PT portal using that candidate's Express Entry profile number and Job seeker validation code. This is where the PT will be prompted to enter the nomination certificate number, date the certificate was issued, date the certificate expires and the date the applicant submitted a complete application for nomination (lock-in date relevant to age of dependents for PNP only). This data is downloaded to the client's Express Entry application in GCMS as PNC details.
4. What do I do if I can't find the nomination when searching the PNC workbooks? Ensure you are searching using the top bar on the main screen of the PNC workbooks! Double check the nomination certificate number. If the nomination certificate number is not generating any hits, try searching the nomination by the client's name (only if the name seems to be unique). Searching the name "Singh", for example, will give you too many results. You can also try searching using the client's Express Entry application number; however, the client may have more than one number if they submitted more than one Express Entry application.
# 260 | P a g e

2A-2024-64537-000260

mmigration, Refugees Immigration, Réfugiés and Citizenship Canadaet Citoyenneté Canada Information disclosed under the Access to Information Act L'information divulguée en vertu de la loi sur l'accés à l’infonmation

If the broad search is not generating any hits, you can also focus on the spreadsheet that should hold the nomination information for your client. In the PNC details, Issuance Date indicates which spreadsheet your client's nomination should be found. (Example: Issuance Date in PNC Details – January 21, 2016→Nomination should be reported on the sheet for January 2016). Hint: This type of search should be done as a last resort as you will not find any possible extensions! If you have thoroughly searched and still no results found, send a PNP Verification Request to the RAs. The reason for your request will be: Unable to locate nomination on spreadsheets.

5. Why is my client's nomination highlighted in YELLOW on the spreadsheet? Provinces must continue to support the nomination in order for the client to be eligible under the Provincial Nominee Program. Provinces will instruct our office to hold processing of the client's application for permanent residence under the Provincial Nominee Program if they are investigating the nomination for eligibility. As provinces cannot change the status of the nomination in the candidate's Express Entry profile, they will send IRCC (our office) an email placing the nomination on hold. Once our office receives this email, the RAs highlight the nomination in yellow and will enter a note in the comments section of the nomination. Note will read as follows: ***Email received 24APR2017 from prov placing nomination on hold (tc) ***. In cases where the nomination is placed on hold, stop processing and send an email to the RAs advising nomination has been placed on hold. Your email to the RA will contain the following information:
# Application #:

## PA name: PA DOB:

# Nomination #:

Prov of Nomination:

Spreadsheet indicates nomination has been placed on hold. Please advise.

6. Why is my client's nomination highlighted in RED on the spreadsheet? Provinces must continue to support the nomination in order for the client to be eligible to apply for permanent residence in Canada under the Provincial Nominee Program. In some instances, provinces will revoke or withdraw a client's nomination. The client will not be eligible for permanent residence under the Provincial Nominee Program if their nomination has been withdrawn by the province. As provinces cannot change the status of the nomination in the candidate's Express Entry profile, they will send IRCC (our office) an email advising the nomination has been revoked or withdrawn. Once our office receives this email, the RAs highlight the nomination in red and will enter a

note in the comments section of the nomination. Note will read as follows: ***Email received 24APR2017 from prov advising nomination has been revoked (or withdrawn) (tc) ***. Once a withdrawal is identified stop processing the application. Promote the application, do not complete the R10 review Refer the file to RC-C07, Enter Parked Case note – Sent to RA for PNP nomination status review Send RA an email using the following template:

# Please review nomination status for EE app

Nomination #: Province of Nomination:

7. How do I send a PNP Verification Request to the RA? Consult your training manual
8. What do I do after I send the PNP Verification Request to the RA? Enter a note in GCMS indicating the file is parked awaiting verification from the province.
9. How do I know if the RA sent my request to the province? The RA will copy you on the email that was forwarded to the province. It is recommended to create a folder in your Microsoft Outlook to hold these emails as you may need to refer to them if you need to send a second request to the RA. [ To create a folder under your Inbox in Microsoft Outlook, simply highlight “Inbox”, right-click and select “New Folder” and name it “Pending PNP Verifications”. When the Resource Agent copies you on the email and it is received in your inbox, drag and drop the email in this folder.] Once you receive a response, be sure to delete the email from this folder as you will no longer need it.
10. Five business days have passed and I haven't received a response to my PNP Verification Request, what do I do now? Once 5 business days (not including weekends or holidays) have passed, you may send a second request. Second requests must be sent to the RA in the following manner:
- Open the email you were copied on that was sent to the province by the RA. This email would have been delivered to your Microsoft Outlook Inbox. This shows the RA the date the initial request was sent to the province. Click “Forward” in the “To” field, enter the RA's email address. In the Subject line, add “2nd Request” to the PNP Verification Request subject that was already present. Click “Send” and keep the file in your code until you have received a response.

## NAMING PROCEDURES: ENTERING/UPDATING NAMES IN GCMS

When entering names in GCMS: 0Verify the Family Name, Given Name(s), Gender, DOB, Country of Birth and, Place of Birth. The information must match the client's passport. Verify the name with the MRZ line of the passport. Set the correct name line as Primary or create a new line if changes are required OThe passport MRZ lines should ALWAYS be used when verifying the client's name. Occasionally there is a variation of the name in the VIZ section of the passport, or they have French accents or accent marks included over the letters. In these instances you must consult the Names Policy. ONames Policy: [http://cicintranet.ci.gc.ca/connexion/tools-outils/id/name-nom/travel-voyage-eng.aspx](http://cicintranet.ci.gc.ca/connexion/tools-outils/id/name-nom/travel-voyage-eng.aspx) [http://cicintranet.ci.gc.ca/connexion/tools-outils/id/name-nom/gcms-smgc-eng.aspx](http://cicintranet.ci.gc.ca/connexion/tools-outils/id/name-nom/gcms-smgc-eng.aspx) o If the passport does not state the Place of Birth for the client, leave what the client has entered into GCMS. Ex: On a Korean passport it does not show a Place of Birth (city), do not change GCMS to say Korea. Place of Birth should never be a country 0To create a new line for the name: Click New Select Primary under Name Type field Tab across to each field and enter the information as per passport Press CTRL+S keys Inde 2 Narres o

If an application includes a change of name, the Program Assistant must: Verify if the name is included under the Names tab If not included, add the name as indicated on the document and select either Former or AKA as the Name Type

FRENCH ACCENTS OR ACCENT MARKS:

If a client has specified a French accent in his/her name, and it is supported by the VIZ of their passport, the name must be recorded in GCMS as the primary name (these will not show in the MRZ).

If a client has specified a French accent in his/her name, and it is NOT supported by the VIZ of their passport (ex: in a document, or letter, etc), it will be recorded as an AKA in GCMS and the primary name will be recorded as per the MRZ.

The names found in the original language should never be recorded as part of the primary name or printed on citizenship and immigration documents, unless they contain French accents, in which case they are recorded in IRCC's system of record and on IRCC issued documents. Where possible, these names will be recorded as an "AKA" (alias) or "other name" in IRCC's system of record, for verification purposes.

GCMS accepts the following French accents: Â-À - É-Ê - Ë - È - 1 - Î - Ô - Ü - ù - Û - Ç.

To produce the necessary characters in capitals for recording and issuing client's name in GCMS, follow “ALT + CODE" key below: ALT +

### NOTES REVIEW QUESTIONS

Each note requires editing prior to being entered in GCMS. The following indicates what items should be included in your note, ask yourself the following questions when doing the review of your note: •Review Type: Is this filled in as Program Assistant?

- Application #: Make sure this is included and matches the file you will be processing
- Regulation: (LINE OF BUSINESS OF THE FILE)
- Min Score: (# pulls from GCMS EE Eligibility tab)
- Existing UCl or New UCl(s)
- Are there Stat Questions on this file, they should be noted here, and who they are for, PA or SP
- Is there a representative/Designated Individual on this file or included with this file? • Are medicals attached?
- Are the medicals expired? Did you include the correct extra note?
- - OR-Were Medicals requested? (appendix MM) Did you include the correct extra note?
- Are fees verified?
- Language test results must be noted for PA, if provided by the SP they must be noted for the SP
- Education must be noted for PA, for Foreign Credential it must be noted EXACTLY as the ECA. Foreign credential must be noted if it is provided or not.
- Canadian education noted? Is it undeterminable? Did you include the edoc#?
- Is there a SP? If so, their education must be noted as well Police certificates must be noted if they are provided whether they are required or not. Refer to the notes section of this manual for guidance, if there are stat questions on this file, the recommendation must be changed to review required
- Did you add the Pending Biometrics RCMP results note as required? •Is the AGE listed?
- Was there any documents set for review such as Client Information or a Client enquiry?
- Did you have any additional notes on the side bar of your ADMM to address before finalizing your note. TIP: ALWAYS review your note prior to adding it to the GCMS Notes tab. Ensure all necessary changes are made and any additional details are added as required.

## REJECTION LETTER INSERTS

Some rejection reasons regarding Police Certificates and Education are already included in the Rejection Letter Template. Note, you may have to adjust the rejection templates below and in the letter template itself to suit the particular item you may be rejecting for, or reference to the spouse or Dependent Child if the document being rejected is for another person on the file.

All letters should use pre-approved blurbs. Program Assistants should not be creating their own letter inserts. If there is a letter template that is required that is not included below – refer to the RA for guidance.

## REJECTION – Academic IELTS Language Test

The IELTS Language test version that you submitted is not acceptable as it is an Academic test. Only General Language test versions are acceptable.

REJECTION – Birth Certificate (translation only provided) You did not provide a copy of your dependent's original birth certificate to accompany the certified translation.

REJECTION-PPT biodata not provided

A copy of the passport/travel document for[NAME]was not provided. The copied pages that were provided did not include the information required-bio data page-which contains passport number, date of issue and expiry, photo, name, date and place of birth, amendments to name date of birth, or expiration.

## REJECTION-PPT expired

The copy of the passport submitted for***NAME***is expired. The expiration date is YYYY/MM/DD. The received date of your application isYYYY/MM/DD.

REJECTION-Divorce/Marriage Certificate translation only or original only

The Marriage Certificate provided was the translated/original version only. You did not provide the translated/original version of the document. If a supporting document is in a language other than English or French, the applicant must provide IRCC with:

- the English or French translation; and an affidavit from the person who completed the translations; and a certified photocopy of the original document.
